The company serves 16,000+ medical practices and 11,000+ physicians across five primary specialties: dermatology, ophthalmology, orthopedics, plastic surgery, and med spa. Nextech positions itself as the leading specialty-specific alternative to generic EHR platforms, winning Best in KLAS for Ambulatory Specialty EHR in 2024 and 2025, and Best in KLAS for Ambulatory Ophthalmology Solutions in 2025 and 2026.\n     9→\n    10→Nextech has been through several private equity transitions: Francisco Partners sold the company to Thomas H. Lee Partners in 2019 for ~$500M, which in turn sold to TPG in 2023 for $1.4B. The company has grown through acquisitions, notably acquiring SRS Health (an orthopedic-focused EHR) in January 2019 and TouchMD (a visual consultation, marketing, and imaging platform for aesthetic/surgical practices) in October 2022. The company also launched an AI-powered documentation tool called Cora Scribe in January 2026 and has a partnership with Ocuco for integrated optical software.\n    11→\n    12→Nextech operates multiple distinct software platforms under its umbrella, which is critical context for understanding their CHPL certifications. Their developer portal lists four separate platforms with their own APIs: IntelleChartPRO (ICP), NexCloud/Select, SRSPro, and Nextech Practice+ PM. The two CHPL-certified products correspond to two of these: \"Nextech EHR (ICP)\" is IntelleChartPRO, and \"SRS EHR\" is SRSPro.\n    13→\n    14→---\n    15→\n    16→## Product: Nextech EHR (ICP) — IntelleChartPRO\n    17→\n    18→CHPL ID: 11724\n    19→CHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.2051.Inte.09.02.0.251202\n    20→Version: 9\n    21→Certification Date: 2025-12-02\n    22→\n    23→### What It Is\n    24→\n    25→IntelleChartPRO (ICP) is Nextech's cloud-based EHR platform. The \"ICP\" in the CHPL listing stands for IntelleChartPRO, confirmed by the developer portal which lists \"ICP (IntelleChartPRO) R4 bundle\" as a FHIR endpoint. It is a full-featured clinical EHR with integrated practice management, designed specifically for specialty medical practices. The platform is cloud-based/SaaS, accessible from any device including iPad and mobile apps.\n    26→\n    27→ICP is certified for a broad set of criteria including clinical data management (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), (b)(10)–(b)(11); patient portal/view-download-transmit (e)(1); clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3); FHIR API access (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10); and syndromic surveillance (f)(5). The (e)(1) certification for patient portal and (g)(10) for Standardized FHIR API are notable — these are present in ICP but absent from SRS EHR.\n    28→\n    29→### Users & Market\n    30→\n    31→IntelleChartPRO serves specialty ambulatory practices, primarily in dermatology, ophthalmology, plastic surgery, orthopedics, and med spa. End users include physicians, clinical staff, billing staff, and practice managers. The platform is used by solo practitioners through multi-site specialty groups. Nextech states its customers include 11,000+ physicians and 60,000+ office staff across their platforms.\n    32→\n    33→The product won Best in KLAS for Ambulatory Specialty EHR (2024, 2025) and Ambulatory Ophthalmology Solutions (2025, 2026). It also holds AAD (American Academy of Dermatology) DataDerm Gold Recognition — a quality registry endorsement specific to dermatology.\n    34→\n    35→### Modules & Functionality\n    36→\n medication, lab, and imaging orders via CPOE\n   188→- **Prescription data** — e-prescribing\n   189→- **Imaging data / PACS** — SRS Health was explicitly described as a PACS solution provider\n messages** — direct messaging (h)(1)\n   197→\n   198→Notably, SRS EHR is NOT certified for (e)(1) patient portal view-download-transmit, which may mean it either lacks a patient portal or relies on a separate system for patient-facing access. It IS certified for (e)(3) patient health information export request, suggesting patients can request data exports but may not have a self-service portal.\n   199→\n   200→---\n   201→\n   202→## Key Observations for EHI Export Assessment\n   203→\n   204→1. **Two distinct platforms**: IntelleChartPRO (ICP) and SRSPro are separate technical platforms with separate APIs, separate CHPL certifications, and likely separate databases. The EHI export documentation at the shared URL may need to cover both platforms distinctly.\n   205→\n   206→2. **A third platform exists**: NexCloud/Select also has FHIR APIs listed on the developer portal but does not appear in the CHPL metadata provided. It's unclear whether NexCloud is a separate product or a rebranded version of one of the others.\n   207→\n   208→3. **Rich data beyond clinical**: Both platforms — but especially ICP — store significant non-clinical data including billing/claims, inventory, CRM/lead management, cosmetic/aesthetic business data, marketing campaign data, patient communications (text messages), and TouchMD visual consultation content. A complete EHI export should account for all of this.\n   209→\n   210→4. **Specialty-specific data**: The products store specialty-specific clinical data (ophthalmology drawings, dermatology images, orthopedic PACS, cosmetic before/after photos) that may require specialized export handling beyond standard C-CDA documents.\n   211→\n   212→5. **Acquisition history creates complexity**: The SRS Health acquisition (2019) and TouchMD acquisition (2022) mean the \"Nextech\" product ecosystem includes components with different technical origins, which may affect how comprehensively data can be exported from a unified system.\n   213→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. The company serves 16,000+ medical practices and 11,000+ physicians across five primary specialties: dermatology, ophthalmology, orthopedics, plastic surgery, and med spa. Nextech positions itself as the leading specialty-specific alternative to generic EHR platforms, winning Best in KLAS for Ambulatory Specialty EHR in 2024 and 2025, and Best in KLAS for Ambulatory Ophthalmology Solutions in 2025 and 2026.\n\nNextech has been through several private equity transitions: Francisco Partners sold the company to Thomas H. Lee Partners in 2019 for ~$500M, which in turn sold to TPG in 2023 for $1.4B. The company has grown through acquisitions, notably acquiring SRS Health (an orthopedic-focused EHR) in January 2019 and TouchMD (a visual consultation, marketing, and imaging platform for aesthetic/surgical practices) in October 2022. The company also launched an AI-powered documentation tool called Cora Scribe in January 2026 and has a partnership with Ocuco for integrated optical software.\n\nNextech operates multiple distinct software platforms under its umbrella, which is critical context for understanding their CHPL certifications. Their developer portal lists four separate platforms with their own APIs: IntelleChartPRO (ICP), NexCloud/Select, SRSPro, and Nextech Practice+ PM. The two CHPL-certified products correspond to two of these: \"Nextech EHR (ICP)\" is IntelleChartPRO, and \"SRS EHR\" is SRSPro.\n\n---\n\n## Product: Nextech EHR (ICP) — IntelleChartPRO\n\nCHPL ID: 11724\nCHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.2051.Inte.09.02.0.251202\nVersion: 9\nCertification Date: 2025-12-02\n\n### What It Is\n\nIntelleChartPRO (ICP) is Nextech's cloud-based EHR platform. The \"ICP\" in the CHPL listing stands for IntelleChartPRO, confirmed by the developer portal which lists \"ICP (IntelleChartPRO) R4 bundle\" as a FHIR endpoint. It is a full-featured clinical EHR with integrated practice management, designed specifically for specialty medical practices. The platform is cloud-based/SaaS, accessible from any device including iPad and mobile apps.\n\nICP is certified for a broad set of criteria including clinical data management (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), (b)(10)–(b)(11); patient portal/view-download-transmit (e)(1); clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3); FHIR API access (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10); and syndromic surveillance (f)(5). The (e)(1) certification for patient portal and (g)(10) for Standardized FHIR API are notable — these are present in ICP but absent from SRS EHR.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nIntelleChartPRO serves specialty ambulatory practices, primarily in dermatology, ophthalmology, plastic surgery, orthopedics, and med spa. End users include physicians, clinical staff, billing staff, and practice managers. The platform is used by solo practitioners through multi-site specialty groups. Nextech states its customers include 11,000+ physicians and 60,000+ office staff across their platforms.\n\nThe product won Best in KLAS for Ambulatory Specialty EHR (2024, 2025) and Ambulatory Ophthalmology Solutions (2025, 2026). It also holds AAD (American Academy of Dermatology) DataDerm Gold Recognition — a quality registry endorsement specific to dermatology.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, product pages, and third-party reviews, IntelleChartPRO includes:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting:**\n- Customizable specialty-specific templates with \"Adaptive Template Technology\" that selects appropriate templates based on patient context and filters out unrelated information\n- Smart stamping for charting efficiency\n- IntelleDraw tool for drawing/annotating directly within the EHR (particularly for ophthalmology)\n- iPad and mobile provider apps for reviewing charts, signing labs, and charting\n- Voice recognition support for documentation\n- AI-powered Cora Scribe ambient documentation (launched January 2026) — listens to natural conversations and generates clinical notes\n\n**e-Prescribing:**\n- Integrated e-prescribing with pharmacy integration\n\n**Orders & Labs:**\n- Lab integration and lab order management\n- Lab result review on mobile devices\n\n**Imaging & Photos:**\n- Photo management with markup/drawing capabilities integrated into the EHR\n- Integration with TouchMD for visual consultation, before/after photos, and patient education imaging\n- Image-intensive workflow support (noted as a strength in reviews for dermatology/plastic surgery/ophthalmology)\n\n**Practice Management & Scheduling:**\n- Advanced scheduling for multi-location, multi-provider operations\n- Appointment management\n- Referral management\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle:**\n- Integrated billing with claims management and coding (EM coding)\n- Nextech Payments — integrated payment processing accepting all forms of payment\n- Point of sale (POS) functionality\n- Recurring transactions and membership program billing\n- Payment plans and automated billing\n- Inventory management with automated planning, purchasing, and tracking\n- ASC (Ambulatory Surgery Center) billing capability\n- MIPS compliance monitoring (customers report ~97% average MIPS scores)\n- Revenue Cycle Management (RCM) services available\n\n**Patient Engagement:**\n- Patient portal with online scheduling, bill pay, and mobile medical record access\n- Two-way texting for patient communication\n- Telehealth capabilities\n- TouchMD integration for visual consultation and patient education\n\n**Specialty-Specific Features:**\n- Cosmetic functionality: quotes, packages, gift cards with tracking\n- Lead management with automated capture, tracking, and marketing campaign tracking\n- Membership and subscription management (med spa/aesthetics)\n- CRM (customer relationship management) tools\n- Regulatory performance monitoring for reimbursement optimization\n\n**Analytics & Reporting:**\n- Advanced analytics and reporting\n- Customizable dashboards\n- Population health data capabilities\n\n**Integrations:**\n- FHIR R4 API (SMART on FHIR compliant)\n- HL7 FHIR Standards API\n- Ocuco integration for optical software (ophthalmology retail/dispensary)\n- Surescripts for e-prescribing (implied by e-prescribing certification)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features described above and the certification criteria, IntelleChartPRO stores and manages:\n\n- **Patient demographics** — confirmed by FHIR API documentation and multiple feature descriptions\n- **Clinical notes and encounter documentation** — extensive charting templates, smart stamping, AI-generated notes (Cora Scribe)\n- **Problem lists, medication lists, allergies** — certified for (a)(1) CPOE meds, (a)(2) CPOE labs, (a)(3) CPOE imaging, (a)(5) demographics, (a)(14) implantable device list\n- **Orders** — medication orders, lab orders, imaging orders (certified for CPOE across all three)\n- **Prescriptions** — e-prescribing data\n- **Lab results** — lab integration with mobile review\n- **Clinical images and photos** — before/after photos, annotated drawings (IntelleDraw), TouchMD visual consultation images\n- **Documents** — document management referenced in feature lists\n- **Scheduling data** — appointments, multi-provider/multi-location scheduling\n- **Billing and claims data** — claims, payment history, POS transactions, recurring billing, membership/subscription billing\n- **Inventory data** — product/supply tracking with automated purchasing\n- **Patient portal content** — patient messages, portal access logs, shared records\n- **Patient communications** — two-way text messages\n- **Quality measures data** — CQM reporting (c)(1)–(c)(3), MIPS scores\n- **Syndromic surveillance data** — certified for (f)(5)\n- **Lead/CRM data** — lead tracking, marketing campaign data, patient relationship management\n- **Cosmetic/aesthetic data** — quotes, packages, gift cards, cosmetic procedure tracking\n- **Telehealth session data** — telehealth visit records\n- **Implantable device data** — certified for (a)(14) implantable device list\n- **Care plan data** — transitions of care documents (b)(1), (b)(2)\n\nThird-party reviews note that the system is strong on image management and specialty-specific documentation, but weaker on billing/claims management (70% of reviewers in one survey reported problems with billing features) and scheduling reliability.\n\n---\n\n## Product: SRS EHR (SRSPro)\n\nCHPL ID: 11040\nCHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.2051.SRSE.12.03.1.221202\nVersion: 12\nCertification Date: 2022-12-02\n\n### What It Is\n\nSRS EHR — also known as SRSPro — is Nextech's second certified EHR platform, originating from SRS Health, which Nextech acquired in January 2019. SRS Health was historically the leading EHR for orthopedic practices, with over 5,000 providers at the time of acquisition. It was also used by ophthalmologists, cardiologists, and other specialists. SRSPro is a distinct platform from IntelleChartPRO, with its own API (Swagger-documented, per the developer portal) and its own CHPL certification.\n\nSRS EHR is certified for clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(9), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), (b)(10); patient request for data export (e)(3) but NOT (e)(1) patient portal view-download-transmit; clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3); immunization/syndromic reporting (f)(1), (f)(5); and API access (g)(2)–(g)(7) but NOT (g)(9)–(g)(10) for Standardized FHIR API. It also has (h)(1) for direct secure messaging. The absence of (e)(1) and (g)(10) suggests SRSPro may not have the same level of patient-facing portal or modern FHIR API as IntelleChartPRO.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nSRS EHR was historically the dominant EHR in orthopedic surgery, and SRS Health was described as \"preeminent among orthopedists\" at the time of acquisition. It also served ophthalmologists, cardiologists, and other surgical specialists. The platform is known for speed and efficiency in high-volume practice settings, with streamlined workflows designed for surgical specialties.\n\nAt the time of the 2019 acquisition, SRS Health had 5,000+ providers. It is unclear how many current SRS EHR users exist vs. how many have migrated to IntelleChartPRO or NexCloud, but the product maintains active CHPL certification (certified 2022).\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nLess specific information is publicly available about SRSPro's feature set compared to IntelleChartPRO, since Nextech's current marketing focuses on the unified \"Nextech\" brand. Based on what was found:\n\n**Clinical Documentation:**\n- EHR with specialty-specific templates\n- Emphasis on speed and efficiency for high-volume surgical practices\n- Clinical workflow tools optimized for orthopedics\n\n**Practice Management:**\n- Scheduling and appointment management\n- PM system with ASC billing capability (per vendor materials about surgical billing)\n\n**Billing:**\n- Billing and claims management\n- PACS (Picture Archiving and Communication System) integration — SRS Health was described as providing \"EHR/EMR, PM, and PACS software solutions\"\n\n**Orders & Prescribing:**\n- CPOE for medications, labs, and diagnostic imaging (certified (a)(1)–(a)(4))\n- e-Prescribing (certified (a)(1))\n\n**Clinical Decision Support:**\n- CDS interventions (certified (a)(9))\n\n**Reporting:**\n- CQM reporting (c)(1)–(c)(3)\n- Immunization reporting (f)(1)\n- Syndromic surveillance (f)(5)\n\n**Data Exchange:**\n- Direct secure messaging (h)(1)\n- Transitions of care (b)(1), (b)(2)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the certification criteria and available product information, SRS EHR stores:\n\n- **Patient demographics** — (a)(5) certified\n- **Clinical documentation** — encounter notes, specialty templates\n- **Problem lists, medication lists, allergies** — standard clinical lists\n- **Orders** — medication, lab, and imaging orders via CPOE\n- **Prescription data** — e-prescribing\n- **Imaging data / PACS** — SRS Health was explicitly described as a PACS solution provider\n- **Lab results** — lab integration\n- **Implantable device data** — (a)(14) certified\n- **Scheduling data** — appointment management\n- **Billing/claims data** — integrated PM billing\n- **Quality measures data** — CQM, immunization, and syndromic reporting\n- **Care transitions documents** — C-CDA documents for transitions of care\n- **Secure messages** — direct messaging (h)(1)\n\nNotably, SRS EHR is NOT certified for (e)(1) patient portal view-download-transmit, which may mean it either lacks a patient portal or relies on a separate system for patient-facing access. It IS certified for (e)(3) patient health information export request, suggesting patients can request data exports but may not have a self-service portal.\n\n---\n\n## Key Observations for EHI Export Assessment\n\n1. **Two distinct platforms**: IntelleChartPRO (ICP) and SRSPro are separate technical platforms with separate APIs, separate CHPL certifications, and likely separate databases. The EHI export documentation at the shared URL may need to cover both platforms distinctly.\n\n2. **A third platform exists**: NexCloud/Select also has FHIR APIs listed on the developer portal but does not appear in the CHPL metadata provided. It's unclear whether NexCloud is a separate product or a rebranded version of one of the others.\n\n3. **Rich data beyond clinical**: Both platforms — but especially ICP — store significant non-clinical data including billing/claims, inventory, CRM/lead management, cosmetic/aesthetic business data, marketing campaign data, patient communications (text messages), and TouchMD visual consultation content. A complete EHI export should account for all of this.\n\n4. **Specialty-specific data**: The products store specialty-specific clinical data (ophthalmology drawings, dermatology images, orthopedic PACS, cosmetic before/after photos) that may require specialized export handling beyond standard C-CDA documents.\n\n5. **Acquisition history creates complexity**: The SRS Health acquisition (2019) and TouchMD acquisition (2022) mean the \"Nextech\" product ecosystem includes components with different technical origins, which may affect how comprehensively data can be exported from a unified system.\n","numLines":213,"startLine":1,"totalLines":213}}}

This ZIP file consists of the\nfollowing folders and files in following structure:\n•\n\n•\n\nPatient Data folder - contains all JSON, XML (CCDA), and PDF EHI files for the patient. Within the\nPatient Data Folder, a subfolder for each type of EHI can be found. Please see below for a description\nof each folder.\nRead Me file - the file containing the public link to the documentation.\n\nZIP file name\nThe ZIP file name consists of:\n\n\f•\n•\n\nPatient Last Name, First Name and Middle Initial.\nPatient’s unique Medical Record Number (MRN)\n\nFor example: LastName_First Name_MiddleInitial_MRN.zip\n\nFolders & Files within the Patient Data folder\nFiles exported within the Patient Data folder are:\nFolder\nAppointments\nClinical Alerts\n\nFile\nAppointments.json\nClinicalAlerts.json\nCommunications.json\nInternalCommunications.json\n\nCommunications\n\nSecureMessages.json\n\nToDos.json\n[Consent Template/Location].pdf\nConsents\n\nDemographics\nDocuments >\nDocument Type >\nDate\n\nExport of Patient Communications –\nIntelleChartPRO only customers\nExport of Patient Internal\nCommunications\nExport of Patient Secure Messages (exist\nfrom the patient portal)\n\nTasks that are stored in the Microservice\ndatabase\nSigned patient consents\n\nEx:\nInformed Consent for Cataract Surgery\n(OD).pdf\nNotes.json\n1. Document file(s) in .jpg format\n2. Metadata file(s) whose name\ncontains the document number in\n.txt format\n2 files may be found in this folder:\n\nEncounters\n(organized by date\nof encounter)\n\nDescription\nExport of all patient appointments\n\n1. CCDA.xml\n2. ChartNote.PDF\n\nPatient Demographics\nDocument file and a text file which\ncontains the corresponding meta data.\nEach encounter is organized in its own\nsubfolder within the Encounters Folder.\nThe individual encounter will be identified\nby\nYear-Month – Date – Encounter Time:\n\nFor Bulk Export, Letters.PDF are also\nincluded (see letters format below)\n\n\fUnsigned encounters will not be\nexported.\n\nYEAR-Month-Day – Time.pdf\n\nSigned patient forms\n\nGlaucomaFlowSheetComments.json\n\nComments from the Glaucoma FlowSheet\n\nForms > Patient\nForms\nGlaucoma Flow\nSheet Comments\nImages > File Type >\nDate\n\nInsurance &\nAuthorization\nIntravitreal\nInjection Log\nComments\n\n1. Image file(s) in .jpg format\n2. Metadata file(s) whose name\ncontains the image number in .txt\nformat\n\nImage file and a text file which contains\nthe corresponding meta data.\n\nInsuranceAuthorization.json\n\nInsurance and Authorization notes\n\nIntravitrealInjectionLogComments.json\n\nComments from the Intravitreal Injection\nLog\n\n[Letter Type]_Date_(Type Count).pdf\n\nSigned letters\n\nEx: Update_2023-11-22(0000).pdf\nLetters\nFor bulk export: [Letter Type].pdf\n\nLaboratories\nProcedures\nReferrals\nRefractions\nShared Care\nComments\nShared Care\nReceived\n\nEx: Update.pdf\nLaboratory.json\nProcedures.json\nReferrals.json\nRefractions.json\nIncomingSharedCareComments.json\nOutgoingSharedCareComments.json\nChart Notes.pdf\n\nOrdered studies including labs and results\nLogged Procedures (procedures,\ndiagnostics, surgeries)\nReferral information (outgoing and\nincoming communication information)\nRefractions\nIncoming comments received through\nShared Care\nOutgoing comments received through\nShared Care\nChart notes received through Shared Care\n\n\fSpecialty\nMedications\nSurgery Planner\nComments\n\nSpecialty Medications.json\n\nExported Specialty Medications\n\nSurgery Planner Comments.pdf\n\nExported Surgery Planner\n\nCCDA file within the ZIP file\nCCDA (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture) is standardized file format used for health information\nexchange, formatted in the XML as defined in the HL7 Implementation Guide.\nhttp://www.hl7.org/ccdasearch/pdfs/Companion_Guide.pdf\nWithin the CCDA following data classes are exported for a given patient:\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n\nAllergies\nChief Complaint\nClinical Notes\nClinical Tests\nDemographics\nDiscussions\nDiagnostic Imaging\nEncounters\nFunctional and Cognitive Status\nHealth Concerns\nHealth Status/Assessment\nImmunizations\nImplantable Devices\nInsurance Coverage\nLaboratory Results\nMedications\nPatient Care Team\nPatient Goals\nPlan of Care\nProblem List\nProcedures\nReason for Referral\nReason for Visit\nSocial Status\nSpecialty Medications\nVitals\n\nAdditional Notes\nExports for Patients without Data\n\n\fAt a minimum, patients must have demographics stored within Nextech EHR to initiate an EHI export. Folders\nfor the following EHI data classes will display in the export by default. If data does not exist for one or more of\nthe data classes, then an empty JSON file will be created.\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n\nAppointments\nCommunications\nDemographics\nGlaucoma Flow Sheet Comments\nInsurance & Authorization\nIntravitreal Injection Log Comments\nProcedures\nReferrals\nRefractions\nSpecialty Medications\n\nExample:\n\n•\n\nFolders for the following EHI and their applicable JSON/XML/PDF files will ONLY be included in the EHI\nexport if there is patient data for the given data class:\no Consents\no Encounters (Chart Notes and CCDA)\no Letters\no Shared Care Comments\no Surgery Planner Comments\n\nSingle Patient EHI Export Auditing\nSingle Patient EHI exports may be initiated within Nextech EHR by any user with permission to do so. When an\nEHI export is requested, the following information will be recorded in the audit log with the date/timestamp\nfor:\n•\n•\n•\n\nPatient the EHI Export is requested for.\nUser initiating EHI export processing event.\nUser downloading the EHI Export\n\n\fBulk Patient EHI Export Auditing\nBulk patient EHI exports are initiated at the request of the client via a support ticket entered by the client or a\nNextech representative. This ticket is associated with the customer’s Salesforce account and includes details\nabout the request of the export including who requested the export, pertinent request and resolution date,\nNextech representatives assigned to the export and any pertinent comments/details entered by the Nextech\nrepresentative.\n\nEncounter Data\nFor each patient encounter we are creating a separate PDF file in the Encounters folder. The format, items,\nfields, and content used in the PDF files, and the order by which this information is presented, is custom and\nthe user/practitioner can use, move, create items and sections based on their preferences and needs for a\nspecific patient.\n\nDocuments and Images Folders\nDocument types may be configured by the practice to display in either the Documents Tab or Images Tab on\nthe Patient Summary Page and may vary practice to practice. All documents attached to the patient’s chart in\nNextech EHR will be exported to the Documents or Images Folder in the EHI ZIP file based on the document\ntype configuration by the practice.\nTypes of documents/images include but are not limited to:\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n\nDiagnostic Test Results/Reports\nPhotos\nOutside Medical Records (Referring Physician Letters, Operative Notes, Hospital Discharge)\nInsurance Cards\nLaboratory Results\nAny additional document attached by the users of the system to the patient record.\n\nNextech Practice Management (Practice+) Documents and Tasks\nEHI exports for Nextech EHR practices also using Nextech’s Practice Management (powered by Practice+)\nsolution will include documents and tasks that are shared between the PM and EHR system when these\nshared features are enabled for the practice. contains all CSV and XML files with patient clinical discrete\ndata including:\no CodeSystemNames.csv - contains all VocabularyID values that are listed across\nother CSV files.\nDocuments folder - contains all documents attached to patient chart.\nEncounter Data Capture folder - contains all Encounter data XML files.\nCCDA file - the standard CCDA file formatted as per Implementation Guide.\nRead Me - the file containing the public link to the documentation.\n\n\fZIP file Name\nThe ZIP file consists of:\n•\n•\n•\n\nPatient First Name Initial and full Last Name\nPatient’s unique Person ID.\nDate and Time stamp when ZIP export is generated.\n\nFor example: FirstNameInitial_LastName_PersonID_DateTimeStamp.ZIP\n\nCSV files within the Clinical Discrete Data folder\n\n\f•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n\nAppointments.csv - export of all patient appointments.\nCustom Alerts.csv - export of all patient custom alerts.\nDiagnosis.csv - export of all patient diagnoses data.\nFamilyHistory.csv - export of full patient family history data.\nFamilyHistoryCodes.csv - export of family history diagnoses codes.\nFamilyHistoryStatuses.csv - export of family history.\nGuarantorInformation.csv - export of patient insurance guarantor information.\nImplantableDevices.csv - export of patient’s all implantable devices data.\nInjections.csv - export of all patient injections.\nInsurance Information.csv - export of patient insurance data.\nOrders.csv - export of all patient orders.\nSmoking Status.csv - export of full patient smoking data.\nUDF.csv - export of all patient UDF data within patient demographic data (UDF = User\nDefined Fields)\nMessages.csv - export of all messages attached to patient chart.\nCodeSystemNames.csv - export of all VocabularyID values.\n\nXML files within the Clinical Discrete Data folder\nXML files exported within the Patient’s clinical discrete data are:\n•\n•\n•\n\nResults.xml - export of all patent’s results data, including laboratory, radiology, and\nprocedure results.\nVitals.xml - export of full patent’s vitals data.\nEncounter.xml - export of full encounter specific data for all patient’s encounters.\n\nCCDA file within the ZIP file\nCCDA (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture) is standardized file format used for health\ninformation exchange, formatted in the XML as defined in the HL7 Implementation Guide.\nWithin the CCDA, the following data classes are exported:\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n\nDemographics (including Related Person Information)\nEncounters\nDiagnoses (Problems)\nVitals\nMedications Administered During Visit\nMedications\nImmunizations\nAllergies\nSocial History\nProcedures\n\n\f•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n•\n\nResults\nCare Team Members\nPlan of Treatment\nHealth Status Assessments (Functional, Mental, Disability Status)\nReason for Referral\nReason for Visit\nPatient Decision Aids and Clinical Instructions\nImplantable Devices\nAssessments\nGoals (including SDOH Goals)\nHealth Concerns\nClinical Notes\nHealth Insurance Information\nSDOH Assessment\n\nClinical Discrete Data Folder: CSV and XML files\nNo data to export in CSV / XML\nIn case of no patient data for a given data class to export, then relevant CSV / XML file will be\ngenerated or not as per these rules:\n•\n•\n\nCSV files: If there is no data for a given data class, then the empty CSV file will be\ncreated.\nXML files:\no Encounter.xml files: if no data to export, no XML file will be created.\no Vitals.xml and Results.xml: if no data to export, empty XML file will be created.\n\nDeleted records in CSV / XML\nSome CSV / XML files will also include the deleted record of the given data class. In such cases,\nthe relevant delete status of the data will be captured within its status as elaborated in the\nData Dictionary.\n\nAdditional Notes\nFamily History CSV files\nWe are exporting separate CSV files that contains patient’s family history data:\n•\n\nFamilyHistory.csv - export of full patient family history data including family history\ndiagnosis data for each family-related person.\n\n\f•\n\n•\n\nFamilyHistoryCodes.csv - export of family history diagnoses codes; the relation between\nrecords in this file and the given family diagnosis in the FamilyHistory.csv is based on\nConceptPath field.\no The ConceptPath is shared between the FamilyHistory and FamilyHistoryCodes\nfiles and can be used to link the data together.\nFamilyHistoryStatuses.csv - export of family history in case No Relevant and Unknown\nFamily History is recorded for that patient.\n\nPatient Demographics\nAll demographic data is included in the CCDA, including insurance information.\n•\n\nPatient demographic data that is non-Insurance related (such as First Last Name, Birth\nDate, Gender, Address….) will not be exported in CSV as all that data is already included\nin CCDA.\n\n•\n\nPatient Insurance Information within the Patient Demographic SRS data should be\nexported in CSV in two separate CSV files:\no Guarantor Information.csv - contains only Guarantor Information from the\npatient insurance data.\no Insurance Information - contains the rest of the patient insurance data.\nAnd with the latest changes it should be included in the Clinical Summary UI in new\nsection “Health Insurance Information” and in the CCDA.\n\n•\n\nNote: Patient Insurance in addition to the Data Dictionary:\nIn the exported CSV file, there are IDs with the following mapping:\n•\n\nCompanyID - it is an internal SRS ID, it is not received in the HL7 file, and this ID is not\n\n•\n\nshown on SRS UI.\nSharedID - this ID is the CompanyID as received inHL7 file and shown on the SRS UI.\n\nCustom Alerts\nCustom alerts are exported to CustomAlerts.csv. This data is not associated with a specific\nencounter or provider. The export contains any active custom alerts based on when the patient\nmeets the alert criteria.\n\nVitals\nVitals data is already provided in the CCDA, but not all fields are included. The export will\ninclude a CSV file that will include all Vitals data for a given patient, including Post Screening\nPlans.\n\n\fSmoking Status\nSmoking Status data is already provided in the CCDA, but not all fields are included. The export\nwill include a CSV file that will include all Smoking Status data for a given patient, including Post\nScreening Plans.\n\nDiagnoses\nDiagnosis data are already provided in the CCDA, but not all fields are included. The export will\ninclude a CSV file that will include all Diagnoses data for a given patient. The Diagnoses.csv will\nhave list of diagnoses agnostic of the patient’s encounters.\n\nNote: Diagnoses in addition to the Data Dictionary:\n1. No Known Diagnoses: when No Known Diagnoses checkbox is selected in SRSPro, this\nwill be only included in the exported CCDA. In this case there will be no Diagnosis.csv file\ncreated within the ZIP export.\n2. No diagnoses recorded: when there are no diagnoses recorded for a given patient and\nNo Known Diagnoses is not selected for that patient, there will be no Diagnosis.csv file\ncreated within the ZIP export.\n3. Type: the type will not be included in the export because there is only one type\n(Diagnosis).\n\nImplantable Devices\nImplantable Device data is already provided in the CCDA, but not all fields are included. The\nexport will include an ImplantableDevices.csv file that will include all Implantable Devices data\nfor a given patient. This file will include all devices: active, inactive and deleted devices.\n\nAppointments List\nList of patient’s appointments will be exported in Appointments.CSV. Also, Reason for visit is\npulled into CCDA. The link between Reason for visit and the appointment is the Date of the\nAppointment.\n\nOrders and Results\nOrders will be exported as CSV and Results will be exported as XML within the same Clinical\nDiscrete Data folder. The relation between Order and Results is defined with RequisitionId that\nis included in each Result. The RequisitionId is the OrderID.\n\nNote: Orders/Results in addition to the Data Dictionary:\n\n\fThe relation between Order and Results is defined with RequisitionId that is included in each\nResult. The RequisitionId is the OrderID.\n\nEncounter Data Capture (XML files per encounter)\nFor each patient encounter we are creating separate Encounter.xml files in the Encounter Data\nCapture folder.\nThe name of the Encounter.XML contains EncounterID (GUID) and Date & Time stamp.\n\nDocuments (folder in addition to CSV files)\nAll documents attached to the patient chart in SRS will be exported in the Documents folder in\nthe EHI ZIP file.\n•\n•\n•\n\nFilename: DocumentName_DocumentId\nDeleted Documents will not be included in the exported documents in EHI Document\nfolder.\nForms-documents, results, TOC Documents, images - all that are considered as\ndocuments will be included in the EHI Export ZIP > Document Folder.\n\n\f•\n\n•\n\n•\n\nMessages will not be included in Documents folder; they’ll be exported as a separate\nCSV file.\no The relation between messages and document is given in the DocumentID\ncolumn listed in the messages.csv file.\nResults received as HL7 files will not be included in Documents folder. HL7 files are used\nonly for data import, and HL7 will not be included in the EHI export as such. Results will\nbe exported as OrdersResults.CSV.\nThe relation between order/result and document is given via UnstructuredDocumentId\ncolumn and corresponding orderID / ResultID listed in the Orders.csv file.\n\nNote: The following files attached to patient’s chart in SRS are considered as\ndocuments and will be exported in the EHI Export ZIP > Document Folder.\n1. Images\n2. Forms - once entered as Form, that form is saved as document and will be included in\nexported files in Documents folder.\n3. Messages - are not considered as documents. Messages, Sticky notes and TOC messages\n(Transfer of Care) are all treated as messages, and as such will not be included in\nDocuments folder. Instead, they are exported in the Messages.csv file.\n4. TOC documents and messages:\na. TOC message is not considered as a document, it’s a message and as such will be\nincluded in the Messages.csv.\nb. TOC attachments (CCDA and Reason for Referral) are considered as documents\nand will be exported in the Documents folder.\n5. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014sWRLbwXSQQL5xhNow4eDL","type":"tool_result","content":"Nextech Select/NexCloud EHI\nExport\nOverview\nThis page provides guidance on the export of Electronic Health Information (EHI) from Nextech\nSelect/NexCloud EMR (aka Select), as per the 170.315(b)(10) standard.\nEHI documentation for Select will consist of:\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nFolder structure description\nData Dictionary of all fields exported within the Clinical Discrete Data\nCSV files within the Clinical Discrete Data (Appointments, Patient demographics, Charges,\nPayments, Custom, Follow up, Notes, Insurances, Recalls, Recalls Steps, Payment Plans,\nPracYakker (Internal messaging), iPad Tasks, iPad Notes)\nEMN folder that will contain all electronic medical notes (encounter data capture) for the\npatient in PDF format\nCCDA - the xml version of the Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture\nPublicly available hyperlink for EHI documentation\n\nNote: All exported data is considered Protected Health Information (PHI) and requires that all\nprivacy and security guidelines be used to ensure that the information is encrypted and stored\nappropriately after download.\n\nNote: This document set includes information on data for all configurations of the certified EMR\nproduct. Exports for customers with a third-party practice management (PM) system will not\ninclude data from that system; customers should contact the administrator of their PM. Nextech\ncustomers who are configured to use the iPad user interface will see limited PM data based on\nwhat is stored in the certified EMR.\n\nData Dictionary\nData dictionary for EHI for Select: Nextech Select/NexCloud EHI Data Dictionary\n\nZip folder Structure\nThe ZIP file consists of following folders and files in this folders structure:\n1\n\n\f\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n2\n\nCSV - folder that contains all CSV files with patient clinical data:\no CSV files: Appointments, Patient demographics, Charges, Payments, Custom\ndata, Follow up, Notes, Insurances, Recall, Recall Steps, Payment Plans,\nPracYakker, iPad Tasks, iPad Notes\nDocuments - folder that contains all documents attached to patient chart\nEMN - folder that contains all encounter data - EMN (electronic medical note) files in\nPDF format\niPad Documents - folder that contains all documents attached to the patient’s chart in\niPad Select application\nCCDA file - the standard CCDA file formatted as per Implementation Guide\nRead me - file that contains the public link to the documentation\n\n\fZIP file name\nThe ZIP file name consists of:\n\n\n\n\nPatient First Name Initial and full Last Name;\nPatient’s unique Person ID;\nDate and Time stamp when ZIP export is generated.\n\nFor example: FirstNameInitial_LastName_PersonID_DateTimeStamp.ZIP\n\n3\n\n\fCSV files within the CSV folder (Clinical Discrete Data)\nCSV files exported within the Patient’s clinical discrete data are:\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nAppointments.csv - export of all patient appointments\nPatient demographics.csv - export all patient demographics data\nCharges.csv - export of all charges data related to the patient\nPayments.csv - export of all payments data related to the patient\nCustomData.csv - export of data added in the system dependent on user’s preferences\n(field’s names and purpose of the fields is custom and it is setup on practice’s level)\nFollow up.csv - export of all follow up/to-do data related to the patient, that a user\nadded as a reminder\nNotes.csv - Export of all notes data related to the patient. Notes can be added from a\nspecific notes section of the product or from other sections of the system such as:\no Notes → Message threads\no EMN → Labs\no Billing\no Payment Plan\no Billing → Add note on existing item on the inside in the hierarchy of bill or\npayment\no EMN → Implantable Devices\no Recalls\no Appointments\no Reminders\no History\nInsurances.csv - export of all patient insurance data\nRecalls.csv- export of all recalls related to the patient\nRecalls Steps.csv - export of all recall steps related to the patient\nPayment plans.csv - export of all payment plans created for the patient\nPracYakker.csv - export of all internal messages where the patient is mentioned\niPad Tasks.csv - export of all tasks related to the patient added on the iPad Select\napplication\niPad Notes.csv- export of all notes related to the patient added on the iPad Select\napplication.\n\nEMN (Electronic Medical Note) - Encounter Data Capture\nFor each patient encounter we are creating a separate EMN.pdf file in the EMN folder. The\nformat, items, fields, and content used in the EMN files, and the order by which this information\nis presented is custom and the user/practitioner can use, move, create items and sections based\non their preferences and needs for a specific patient. In the data fields dictionary document, the\ndocumentation provided for this export is best practice but that does not mean that those fields\nwill be present for every patient nor that those are the only fields that can be included in the\n4\n\n\fexport. That is just a portion of the fields and items used, and the number of fields/items can be\nlarger/smaller totally dependent on user’s preferences,\nThe name of the EMN.pdf contains the Document ID.\n\nCCDA file within the ZIP file\nCCDA (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture) is standardized file format used for health\ninformation exchange in the US, formatted in the XML as defined in the HL7 Implementation\nGuide here: http://www.hl7.org/ccdasearch/pdfs/Companion_Guide.pdf HL7 Standards Product\nBrief - HL7 CDA® R2 IG: C-CDA Templates for Clinical Notes R2.1 Companion Guide, Release 4.1\n- US Realm | HL7 International\nWithin the CCDA following data classes are exported for a given patient:\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n5\n\nPatient Demographics - some patient demographics data is not exported in the CCDA,\nfull patient demographics data is exported in PatientDemographics.csv\nHealth Insurance – some insurance data is not exported in the csv file but that is already\npresent in the CCDA\nAllergies\nMedications\nMedications administered\nProblems\no SDOH Problems\nProblem list\nProcedures\no SDOH Intervention\no Reason for Referral\nClinical Test\nLab orders\nLab results\nDiagnostic Imaging\nImmunizations\nMedical equipment\nAssessment and Plan of Treatment\no SDOH Assessment\nSocial history\nVital signs - some vitals data are exported in the CCDA. In\naddition to that, full diagnoses data is exported in the EMN data export\nReferring Provider\nDischarge summary\n\nGeneral notes on CSV files included in the Clinical Discrete Data folder\nNo data to export in CSV\nIn case of no patient data for a given data class to export, then relevant CSV file will be\ngenerated or not as per these rules:\n\n\nCSV files: If there is no data for a given data class, then the empty CSV file will be\ncreated.\n\nDeleted records in CSV\nSome CSV files will also include the deleted record of the given data class. In such cases, the\nrelevant deleted status of the data will be captured within its status as elaborated in the Data\nDictionary.\ne.g.: Direct messages exchanged between users of the application that are related to a patient\nwill be exported, including the deleted messages (PracYakker.csv export).\n\nInactive patients\nAll patients and patients' data will be exported, including inactive patients.\n\nDocuments (folder)\nAll documents attached to the patient’s chart in Select will be exported to the Documents folder\nin the EHI ZIP file. Billing data is part of\nthe Charges exports. The logic behind is that every charge will have a bill, but one charge or one\nbill can have multiple payments and thus the separation of the charges and payments exports in\nseparate files.\nPayment plans are a separate export because plans are added separately and having a payment\nplan is not required for the patient to be charged, billed, or to make a payment. However, if a\npayment is made it can be linked to a specific payment plan, then that information is part of the\nPayments export. No redirects.\n\n2. **Page fetch**: `curl -sL \"https://www.nextech.com/compliance/ehi-export-documentation\" -o /tmp/nextech-ehi-page.html` — 92KB static HTML page. No JavaScript rendering required to see content.\n\n3. **Content examination**: The page is a simple compliance hub titled \"Cures Update 170.315(b)(10) — Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export Documentation.\" It states: \"Documentation on the §170.315(b)(10) Electronic Health Information export format for each Nextech ONC-certified solution is listed below or will be provided soon.\"\n\n4. **Documentation links found**: Six direct download links organized by platform:\n   - **IntelleChartPro**: PDF export doc + XLSX data dictionary\n   - **Nextech Select/NexCloud**: PDF export doc + XLSX data dictionary\n   - **SRSPro**: PDF export doc + XLSX data dictionary\n\n5. **Downloads**: All six files downloaded successfully via direct curl with User-Agent header. All verified as correct file types (`file` command confirmed PDF documents and Excel 2007+ files).\n\n6. **URL extraction from PDFs**: Only external link found was `http://www.hl7.org/ccdasearch/pdfs/Companion_Guide.pdf` (HL7 standard — not downloaded per instructions). No embedded attachments in any PDF.\n\n7. **Full-page screenshot** captured via Chrome DevTools for visual reference.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nNextech provides **genuine (b)(10) EHI export documentation** for three distinct platforms, each with its own export format, folder structure, and data dictionary. This is notably one of the better-documented EHI export implementations encountered.\n\n### Platform 1: IntelleChartPRO (ICP) — CHPL 11724\n\n**Export format**: Per-patient ZIP file containing:\n- **JSON files** for structured clinical data (appointments, demographics, insurance, labs, procedures, referrals, refractions, specialty medications, etc.)\n- **XML (C-CDA)** for standardized clinical summary per encounter\n- **PDF files** for chart notes, consent forms, letters, surgery planner comments\n- **JPG + TXT metadata** for documents and images\n- A ReadMe file with public documentation link\n\n**Folder structure**: Well-organized by data domain — `Patient Data/Appointments/`, `Patient Data/Encounters/YYYY-MM-DD-Time/`, `Patient Data/Documents/DocumentType/Date/`, `Patient Data/Images/FileType/Date/`, etc.\n\n**Data dictionary**: 19 entities, 341 fields. Notable entities include Patient Demographics (71 fields), ChartNote (49 fields), Tasking_Microservice (46 fields), Refractions (31 fields), Insurance & Auth (22 fields).\n\n**Specialty-specific content**: Glaucoma Flow Sheet Comments, Refractions, Intravitreal Injection Log Comments, Surgery Planner Comments — these reflect Nextech's ophthalmology focus and demonstrate export of specialty clinical data beyond standard USCDI.\n\n**Export scope**: Single patient and bulk patient exports are supported. Bulk exports include Letters in a different format. Only signed encounters are exported. Empty JSON files are created for domains with no data, ensuring consistent structure.\n\n### Platform 2: SRSPro — CHPL 11040\n\n**Export format**: Per-patient ZIP file containing:\n- **CSV files** for structured clinical data (15 CSV types: appointments, custom alerts, diagnoses, family history × 3, guarantor info, implantable devices, injections, insurance, orders, smoking status, UDFs, messages, code system names)\n- **XML files** for encounter data capture (per encounter), vitals, and results\n- **C-CDA XML** for standardized clinical summary\n- **Documents folder** with all chart-attached documents\n- A ReadMe file\n\n**Data dictionary**: 19 entities, 427 fields. Notable entities include Orders (86 fields with detailed CPOE/HL7 mapping), Smoking Status (58 fields including screening plans), Insurance Information (38 fields), Implantable Devices (35 fields), Injections (36 fields), Diagnoses (35 fields).\n\n**Relationship documentation**: SRSPro's docs go further than most in documenting inter-entity relationships — Orders link to Results via RequisitionId, Messages link to Documents via DocumentID, FamilyHistory links to FamilyHistoryCodes via ConceptPath, Orders link to Documents via UnstructuredDocumentId. This is critical for reconstructing a patient record from the export.\n\n**Data handling notes**: Includes deleted records with status flags, handles \"No Known Diagnoses\" correctly, includes GUID-based encounter identifiers, and documents which data is duplicated between CSV/XML and CCDA (vitals, smoking status, diagnoses are in both but with different field sets).\n\n### Platform 3: Nextech Select/NexCloud\n\n**Export format**: Per-patient ZIP file containing:\n- **CSV files** for clinical and financial data (16 CSV types: appointments, demographics, charges, payments, custom data, follow-ups, notes, insurances, recalls × 2, payment plans, PracYakker internal messages, iPad tasks, iPad notes)\n- **EMN (Electronic Medical Note) PDFs** — one per encounter\n- **Documents folder** — all chart-attached documents\n- **iPad Documents folder** — documents from iPad application\n- **C-CDA XML**\n- A ReadMe file\n\n**Data dictionary**: 16 entities, 730 fields — the most detailed of the three. Notable entities include Patient Demographics (212 fields!), Charges (140 fields), Insurances (85 fields), Appointments (52 fields), Payments (50 fields).\n\n**Billing coverage**: The Select/NexCloud export stands out for explicit billing data inclusion — Charges, Payments, and Payment Plans are separate CSV exports with documented relationships (linked via Bill ID and Payment Plan ID). The documentation explicitly notes that practices with iPad-only PM don't include billing data since it's stored in the non-certified PM module.\n\n**Custom data**: Exports practice-configurable custom fields (date, free text, list item, checkbox types) with actual field names as configured by the practice.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nNextech's EHI export documentation represents a **serious and thorough approach to (b)(10) compliance**. This is clearly not a repackaged FHIR API — it's purpose-built export functionality that goes well beyond USCDI.\n\n**Clearly covered domains across platforms:**\n- Patient demographics (extensive — 71-212 fields depending on platform)\n- Clinical encounters/chart notes (as PDFs or XML, per encounter)\n- Problem lists/diagnoses (with full coding detail)\n- Medications (specialty medications in ICP, CCDA medications across all)\n- Allergies (via CCDA)\n- Lab results (JSON in ICP, XML in SRSPro, via CCDA in Select)\n- Orders (extremely detailed in SRSPro — 86 fields)\n- Immunizations (via CCDA)\n- Vital signs (JSON/XML + CCDA)\n- Procedures (separate exports + CCDA)\n- Insurance information (comprehensive — 22-85 fields)\n- Referrals (detailed in ICP)\n- Documents/images (exported as files with metadata)\n- Patient communications (secure messages, internal messages, PracYakker)\n- Implantable devices (CSV + CCDA)\n- Family history (detailed in SRSPro with linked code tables)\n- Consents (signed PDF forms in ICP)\n- Care plans/goals (via CCDA)\n- Social history / SDOH (via CCDA, including SDOH Assessment in SRSPro, SDOH Problems/Goals/Interventions in Select)\n- Clinical notes by type (progress notes, H&P, discharge summaries via CCDA)\n- Smoking status (extensive — 58 fields in SRSPro)\n- Custom/user-defined fields (UDFs in SRSPro, Custom Data in Select)\n- Billing: Charges, Payments, Payment Plans (Select/NexCloud)\n\n**Specialty-specific data covered (ICP):**\n- Glaucoma Flow Sheet Comments\n- Refractions (31 fields)\n- Intravitreal Injection Log Comments\n- Surgery Planner Comments\n- Specialty Medications\n\n**Domains with potential gaps or ambiguity:**\n- **Billing in ICP and SRSPro**: The Select/NexCloud platform explicitly exports charges, payments, and payment plans. However, the ICP and SRSPro export documentation makes no mention of billing/charges/payments data. Given that ICP has integrated billing features (EM coding, claims management, POS, payment processing, etc.), the absence of billing data from ICP's export is a notable gap. SRSPro also has PM billing but no billing CSV.\n- **Inventory data**: ICP is described as having inventory management (automated planning, purchasing, tracking) — this is not mentioned in any export.\n- **Lead/CRM data**: ICP includes lead management, marketing campaign tracking, and CRM tools. None of this appears in the export. While CRM/lead data may not be part of the designated record set in most cases, patient-linked communications could be.\n- **Cosmetic/aesthetic data**: ICP supports quotes, packages, gift cards — none exported. Cosmetic quotes and packages linked to patients could arguably be part of the billing record.\n- **TouchMD visual consultation content**: Not mentioned in exports. If TouchMD images/consultation data is integrated into the patient chart, it should be exported.\n- **Telehealth session data**: No mention of telehealth-specific data in any export (session records, virtual visit metadata).\n- **E-prescribing data**: While medications appear in CCDA, there's no mention of detailed prescription/e-prescribing records (pharmacy selections, fill history, prior authorizations).\n- **Clinical images in SRSPro**: SRSPro's PACS integration suggests image-heavy workflows, but the export documentation doesn't specifically address PACS images vs. chart-attached documents.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **hybrid approach** that is well-suited to the (b)(10) requirement:\n\n- **C-CDA XML** provides standardized representation of core clinical data (allergies, medications, problems, labs, vitals, immunizations, procedures, etc.) — this covers the USCDI slice in a standard format.\n- **Platform-specific structured data** (JSON for ICP, CSV for SRSPro and Select) captures data that doesn't fit in C-CDA — appointments, insurance, specialty clinical data, communications, billing, custom fields.\n- **PDFs** for chart notes/encounters — these preserve the visual layout that clinicians created, though they are less computable.\n- **Document/image files** with metadata — the actual attachments.\n\nThis is a thoughtful design. The C-CDA handles standardized clinical data, while the JSON/CSV files handle everything else. The documentation explicitly notes where data overlaps between C-CDA and structured files (e.g., vitals, diagnoses, smoking status appear in both, with the structured export having more fields).\n\n**Relationship documentation** is a strength, particularly in SRSPro — the docs explain how to join Orders to Results (via RequisitionId), Messages to Documents (via DocumentID), and Family History to Codes (via ConceptPath). This is essential for a third party to reconstruct the patient record.\n\n**Format appropriateness**: Good. JSON/CSV/XML are computable and parseable. PDFs for encounters are less ideal but reflect the reality that encounter notes are highly customizable and layout-dependent. The ZIP structure with consistent folder naming makes the export self-describing.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Readable and navigable**: Yes. Each platform has a clear PDF explaining the export structure, followed by an XLSX data dictionary with per-entity sheets. The documentation is well-organized.\n\n**Field-level definitions**: Present across all three dictionaries but vary in depth:\n- Select/NexCloud is the most detailed (730 fields, with notes on data types, relationships, and edge cases)\n- SRSPro has good field-level detail especially for complex entities (orders, encounter XML)\n- ICP is the least detailed of the three (341 fields) but still provides field names, descriptions, and notes\n\n**Data types**: Not formally specified (no column for data type in any dictionary). You can infer types from descriptions and notes, but there are no explicit type declarations (string, integer, date, boolean, etc.).\n\n**Value sets / coded fields**: Partially documented. SRSPro includes a CodeSystemNames.csv mapping VocabularyIDs to OIDs. The ICP dictionary occasionally notes enumerated values in the \"Note\" column (e.g., \"Routine, High, Urgent\" for priority). But systematic value set documentation is lacking across all three platforms.\n\n**Worked examples / sample data**: The ICP PDF includes a screenshot of an example ZIP folder structure. No sample export files or example data records are provided for any platform.\n\n**Developer implementability**: A developer could implement a basic import from these exports using the documentation — the folder structures are clear, the field names are descriptive, and the relationships are documented (especially in SRSPro). However, the lack of formal schemas (JSON Schema, XSD, OpenAPI), data type specifications, and sample data means there would be significant trial-and-error required.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level granularity**: Field names and descriptions for every exported field across all three platforms. No redirects.\n\n2. **Page fetch**: `curl -sL \"https://www.nextech.com/compliance/ehi-export-documentation\" -o /tmp/nextech-ehi-page.html` — 92KB static HTML page. No JavaScript rendering required to see content.\n\n3. **Content examination**: The page is a simple compliance hub titled \"Cures Update 170.315(b)(10) — Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export Documentation.\" It states: \"Documentation on the §170.315(b)(10) Electronic Health Information export format for each Nextech ONC-certified solution is listed below or will be provided soon.\"\n\n4. **Documentation links found**: Six direct download links organized by platform:\n   - **IntelleChartPro**: PDF export doc + XLSX data dictionary\n   - **Nextech Select/NexCloud**: PDF export doc + XLSX data dictionary\n   - **SRSPro**: PDF export doc + XLSX data dictionary\n\n5. **Downloads**: All six files downloaded successfully via direct curl with User-Agent header. No embedded attachments in any PDF.\n\n7. **Full-page screenshot** captured via Chrome DevTools for visual reference.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nNextech provides **genuine (b)(10) EHI export documentation** for three distinct platforms, each with its own export format, folder structure, and data dictionary. This is notably one of the better-documented EHI export implementations encountered.\n\n### Platform 1: IntelleChartPRO (ICP) — CHPL 11724\n\n**Export format**: Per-patient ZIP file containing:\n- **JSON files** for structured clinical data (appointments, demographics, insurance, labs, procedures, referrals, refractions, specialty medications, etc.)\n- **XML (C-CDA)** for standardized clinical summary per encounter\n- **PDF files** for chart notes, consent forms, letters, surgery planner comments\n- **JPG + TXT metadata** for documents and images\n- A ReadMe file with public documentation link\n\n**Folder structure**: Well-organized by data domain — `Patient Data/Appointments/`, `Patient Data/Encounters/YYYY-MM-DD-Time/`, `Patient Data/Documents/DocumentType/Date/`, `Patient Data/Images/FileType/Date/`, etc.\n\n**Data dictionary**: 19 entities, 341 fields. Notable entities include Patient Demographics (71 fields), ChartNote (49 fields), Tasking_Microservice (46 fields), Refractions (31 fields), Insurance & Auth (22 fields).\n\n**Specialty-specific content**: Glaucoma Flow Sheet Comments, Refractions, Intravitreal Injection Log Comments, Surgery Planner Comments — these reflect Nextech's ophthalmology focus and demonstrate export of specialty clinical data beyond standard USCDI.\n\n**Export scope**: Single patient and bulk patient exports are supported. Bulk exports include Letters in a different format. Only signed encounters are exported. Empty JSON files are created for domains with no data, ensuring consistent structure.\n\n### Platform 2: SRSPro — CHPL 11040\n\n**Export format**: Per-patient ZIP file containing:\n- **CSV files** for structured clinical data (15 CSV types: appointments, custom alerts, diagnoses, family history × 3, guarantor info, implantable devices, injections, insurance, orders, smoking status, UDFs, messages, code system names)\n- **XML files** for encounter data capture (per encounter), vitals, and results\n- **C-CDA XML** for standardized clinical summary\n- **Documents folder** with all chart-attached documents\n- A ReadMe file\n\n**Data dictionary**: 19 entities, 427 fields. Notable entities include Orders (86 fields with detailed CPOE/HL7 mapping), Smoking Status (58 fields including screening plans), Insurance Information (38 fields), Implantable Devices (35 fields), Injections (36 fields), Diagnoses (35 fields).\n\n**Relationship documentation**: SRSPro's docs go further than most in documenting inter-entity relationships — Orders link to Results via RequisitionId, Messages link to Documents via DocumentID, FamilyHistory links to FamilyHistoryCodes via ConceptPath, Orders link to Documents via UnstructuredDocumentId. This is critical for reconstructing a patient record from the export.\n\n**Data handling notes**: Includes deleted records with status flags, handles \"No Known Diagnoses\" correctly, includes GUID-based encounter identifiers, and documents which data is duplicated between CSV/XML and CCDA (vitals, smoking status, diagnoses are in both but with different field sets).\n\n### Platform 3: Nextech Select/NexCloud\n\n**Export format**: Per-patient ZIP file containing:\n- **CSV files** for clinical and financial data (16 CSV types: appointments, demographics, charges, payments, custom data, follow-ups, notes, insurances, recalls × 2, payment plans, PracYakker internal messages, iPad tasks, iPad notes)\n- **EMN (Electronic Medical Note) PDFs** — one per encounter\n- **Documents folder** — all chart-attached documents\n- **iPad Documents folder** — documents from iPad application\n- **C-CDA XML**\n- A ReadMe file\n\n**Data dictionary**: 16 entities, 730 fields — the most detailed of the three. Notable entities include Patient Demographics (212 fields!), Charges (140 fields), Insurances (85 fields), Appointments (52 fields), Payments (50 fields).\n\n**Billing coverage**: The Select/NexCloud export stands out for explicit billing data inclusion — Charges, Payments, and Payment Plans are separate CSV exports with documented relationships (linked via Bill ID and Payment Plan ID). The documentation explicitly notes that practices with iPad-only PM don't include billing data since it's stored in the non-certified PM module.\n\n**Custom data**: Exports practice-configurable custom fields (date, free text, list item, checkbox types) with actual field names as configured by the practice.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nNextech's EHI export documentation represents a **serious and thorough approach to (b)(10) compliance**. This is clearly not a repackaged FHIR API — it's purpose-built export functionality that goes well beyond USCDI.\n\n**Clearly covered domains across platforms:**\n- Patient demographics (extensive — 71-212 fields depending on platform)\n- Clinical encounters/chart notes (as PDFs or XML, per encounter)\n- Problem lists/diagnoses (with full coding detail)\n- Medications (specialty medications in ICP, CCDA medications across all)\n- Allergies (via CCDA)\n- Lab results (JSON in ICP, XML in SRSPro, via CCDA in Select)\n- Orders (extremely detailed in SRSPro — 86 fields)\n- Immunizations (via CCDA)\n- Vital signs (JSON/XML + CCDA)\n- Procedures (separate exports + CCDA)\n- Insurance information (comprehensive — 22-85 fields)\n- Referrals (detailed in ICP)\n- Documents/images (exported as files with metadata)\n- Patient communications (secure messages, internal messages, PracYakker)\n- Implantable devices (CSV + CCDA)\n- Family history (detailed in SRSPro with linked code tables)\n- Consents (signed PDF forms in ICP)\n- Care plans/goals (via CCDA)\n- Social history / SDOH (via CCDA, including SDOH Assessment in SRSPro, SDOH Problems/Goals/Interventions in Select)\n- Clinical notes by type (progress notes, H&P, discharge summaries via CCDA)\n- Smoking status (extensive — 58 fields in SRSPro)\n- Custom/user-defined fields (UDFs in SRSPro, Custom Data in Select)\n- Billing: Charges, Payments, Payment Plans (Select/NexCloud)\n\n**Specialty-specific data covered (ICP):**\n- Glaucoma Flow Sheet Comments\n- Refractions (31 fields)\n- Intravitreal Injection Log Comments\n- Surgery Planner Comments\n- Specialty Medications\n\n**Domains with potential gaps or ambiguity:**\n- **Billing in ICP and SRSPro**: The Select/NexCloud platform explicitly exports charges, payments, and payment plans. However, the ICP and SRSPro export documentation makes no mention of billing/charges/payments data. Given that ICP has integrated billing features (EM coding, claims management, POS, payment processing, etc.), the absence of billing data from ICP's export is a notable gap. SRSPro also has PM billing but no billing CSV.\n- **Inventory data**: ICP is described as having inventory management (automated planning, purchasing, tracking) — this is not mentioned in any export.\n- **Lead/CRM data**: ICP includes lead management, marketing campaign tracking, and CRM tools. None of this appears in the export. While CRM/lead data may not be part of the designated record set in most cases, patient-linked communications could be.\n- **Cosmetic/aesthetic data**: ICP supports quotes, packages, gift cards — none exported. Cosmetic quotes and packages linked to patients could arguably be part of the billing record.\n- **TouchMD visual consultation content**: Not mentioned in exports. If TouchMD images/consultation data is integrated into the patient chart, it should be exported.\n- **Telehealth session data**: No mention of telehealth-specific data in any export (session records, virtual visit metadata).\n- **E-prescribing data**: While medications appear in CCDA, there's no mention of detailed prescription/e-prescribing records (pharmacy selections, fill history, prior authorizations).\n- **Clinical images in SRSPro**: SRSPro's PACS integration suggests image-heavy workflows, but the export documentation doesn't specifically address PACS images vs. chart-attached documents.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **hybrid approach** that is well-suited to the (b)(10) requirement:\n\n- **C-CDA XML** provides standardized representation of core clinical data (allergies, medications, problems, labs, vitals, immunizations, procedures, etc.) — this covers the USCDI slice in a standard format.\n- **Platform-specific structured data** (JSON for ICP, CSV for SRSPro and Select) captures data that doesn't fit in C-CDA — appointments, insurance, specialty clinical data, communications, billing, custom fields.\n- **PDFs** for chart notes/encounters — these preserve the visual layout that clinicians created, though they are less computable.\n- **Document/image files** with metadata — the actual attachments.\n\nThis is a thoughtful design. The C-CDA handles standardized clinical data, while the JSON/CSV files handle everything else. The documentation explicitly notes where data overlaps between C-CDA and structured files (e.g., vitals, diagnoses, smoking status appear in both, with the structured export having more fields).\n\n**Relationship documentation** is a strength, particularly in SRSPro — the docs explain how to join Orders to Results (via RequisitionId), Messages to Documents (via DocumentID), and Family History to Codes (via ConceptPath). This is essential for a third party to reconstruct the patient record.\n\n**Format appropriateness**: Good. JSON/CSV/XML are computable and parseable. PDFs for encounters are less ideal but reflect the reality that encounter notes are highly customizable and layout-dependent. The ZIP structure with consistent folder naming makes the export self-describing.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Readable and navigable**: Yes. Each platform has a clear PDF explaining the export structure, followed by an XLSX data dictionary with per-entity sheets. The documentation is well-organized.\n\n**Field-level definitions**: Present across all three dictionaries but vary in depth:\n- Select/NexCloud is the most detailed (730 fields, with notes on data types, relationships, and edge cases)\n- SRSPro has good field-level detail especially for complex entities (orders, encounter XML)\n- ICP is the least detailed of the three (341 fields) but still provides field names, descriptions, and notes\n\n**Data types**: Not formally specified (no column for data type in any dictionary). You can infer types from descriptions and notes, but there are no explicit type declarations (string, integer, date, boolean, etc.).\n\n**Value sets / coded fields**: Partially documented. SRSPro includes a CodeSystemNames.csv mapping VocabularyIDs to OIDs. The ICP dictionary occasionally notes enumerated values in the \"Note\" column (e.g., \"Routine, High, Urgent\" for priority). But systematic value set documentation is lacking across all three platforms.\n\n**Worked examples / sample data**: The ICP PDF includes a screenshot of an example ZIP folder structure. No sample export files or example data records are provided for any platform.\n\n**Developer implementability**: A developer could implement a basic import from these exports using the documentation — the folder structures are clear, the field names are descriptive, and the relationships are documented (especially in SRSPro). However, the lack of formal schemas (JSON Schema, XSD, OpenAPI), data type specifications, and sample data means there would be significant trial-and-error required.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level granularity**: Field names and descriptions for every exported field across all three platforms. This is above average for EHI export documentation.\n\n**Cardinality**: Not documented. Whether a field is required, optional, or repeating is not specified.\n\n**Versioning**: No version numbers on the data dictionaries. The PDFs have dates in filenames (10.30.25, 2025). No change history.\n\n**Cross-platform consistency**: The three platforms use different export formats (JSON vs CSV vs XML for the same data types), different field naming conventions, and different entity structures. This reflects the reality that they are distinct software systems with separate databases, but it means there is no unified \"Nextech EHI export format\" — each platform must be understood independently.\n\n## Summary Assessment\n\nNextech's EHI export documentation is **well above average** for the (b)(10) requirement. Key strengths:\n\n1. **Genuine (b)(10) scope**: This is clearly not a (g)(10) FHIR API relabeled. The exports include data far beyond USCDI — insurance details, specialty clinical data (glaucoma, refractions, injections), communications, custom fields, and (for Select) billing data.\n\n2. **Three-platform coverage**: Documentation covers all three certified platforms (ICP, SRSPro, Select/NexCloud) with platform-specific detail, acknowledging their distinct architectures.\n\n3. **Structured + standard hybrid**: The C-CDA plus JSON/CSV/XML approach is smart — it gives you standardized data where standards exist and raw structured data where they don't.\n\n4. **Relationship documentation**: Especially in SRSPro, the documentation explains how to join related data across files — this is rare and valuable.\n\n5. **Realistic scope notes**: The documentation acknowledges that encounter note formats are customizable and practice-dependent, that iPad practices have different data boundaries, and that PM-only data isn't in the EHR export. This honesty helps consumers understand what they're working with.\n\nThe main weakness is the **absence of billing data from ICP and SRSPro exports**, despite both platforms having integrated billing. The Select/NexCloud platform shows Nextech knows how to export billing (Charges: 140 fields, Payments: 50 fields), so the gap in ICP and SRSPro appears to be an implementation gap rather than a design decision. There are also no formal schemas, no sample data files, and no data type specifications — the documentation tells you what fields exist but not precisely how to parse them programmatically.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.nextech.com/compliance/ehi-export-documentation\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (all 6 files linked from single page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (standard User-Agent sufficient)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\nNone.
